## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

## 3. The bakeoff protocol

Shared with Snapback 3; LLP 2000 or a sibling record should adopt the same
text so that the two tracks are measured by one instrument.

**Brief.** [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md)
(Clique) M1–M8, plus
[`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md).
The field-work application (LLP 2000 §4) tests generality after the first
slice, on whichever tracks survive the clock.

**Fixture.** The Clique generator at the instrument freeze LLP 1023 and
LLP 1024 record (`f40559f37722c79178be664a11f8a6485f1508fd`): hub `u0001`
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
messages of the retained 0.0.25 replay database. The same fixture for every
track and for the control.

**Harness and judge.** [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md);
the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,
muse-spark); Convex as the control arm; published packages; fresh cold
agents with only the installed package and the task. Neither lead runs the
comparison or grades their own track. The author rules on the numbers.

**Decision rule, pre-registered.**

| Gate | Threshold | How measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loop bar | Fresh agent, published package, reduced Clique M1–M3, ≤ 1,200 s, ≤ 1 Snapback-attributed correction loop, N ≥ 3 | The existing loop-bar protocol (`rules/NOT-DOING.md` §"The bars") |
| Hub acquisition | Full durable content plus first offline thread and search on `u0001`, within 2× the measured physical floor | Floors recorded in the journal in week 1 (§5); the same script on every track |
| Client bytes | Online tier ≤ Convex's shipped React client; the local-first tier's JavaScript ≤ PowerSync's shipped web client, with the wasm device measured beside it and its own budget set by the diet (§7, 2026-09-13: one implementation) | Measured on one pinned toolchain, recorded once in the journal, never assumed |
| Scenarios | LLP 2000 §4's six scenarios green under the simulator on the hub fixture; LLP 2000 §10's outcomes 1–5 and LLP 2000.000 §11's 6–7 | The simulator run record plus the device witnesses |
| Interaction budgets | LLP 2000 §7's targets | Recorded beside the gates; descriptive, not gating, in slice one |
| Holistic score | Three-scorer Clique score with the Convex control | Reported; descriptive, not gating |

A track that passes every gate is a candidate for promotion. A track that
fails the loop bar or acquisition is killed at the clock unless the author
rules otherwise. A mixed result names the gains and the remaining costs, and
the author decides which substrates move into a survivor — which may be
Snapback 2 (LLP 2000.000 §13).

**Clock and checkpoints.** Week 1: floors, experience prototype, simulator
skeleton, first publish. Week 2: server core, partition stream, meter,
simulator invariants. Week 3: web replica, outbox, local queries, the chat
app on the real client. Week 4: Expo, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop
# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

- **The whole application is the unit.** §3's four properties (composition,
  locality, continuity, performance) are the right acceptance vocabulary.
  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
  document. §8 below turns its fourth row into a fixed vocabulary.
- **Composed reads must not fabricate.** Unknown totals stay unknown; revoked
  rows do not linger. Both flips below preserve this; they change what is
  *known*, not what is *claimed*.
- **Writes have one lifecycle with durable admission distinct from commit
  and stable operation identity.** Keep LLP 1018.004's three outcomes.
- **Rust and SQLite.** Yes, and the engine's transaction, interpreter, rule
  evaluation and successor logic are the pieces to copy first (§5).
- **Prototype the experience before the core.** Yes — and §8 makes the
  prototype's API signatures binding on the implementation, not the reverse.
- **Deterministic simulation early**, citing TigerBeetle. This is the single
  most important sentence in LLP 2000 and §11 makes it the primary harness.
- **A real deployment and a native client early.** Yes; §11 puts the native
  client in slice one.
- **Parallel, fenced, 2000-series, promotion is the author's later call.** Yes.

## 4. Where LLP 2000 is wrong or leaves the wrong things open

### 4.1 It reopens the language and leaves the bound model open; invert that

§6 asks to "evaluate Quarry" and "evaluate any alternative against the same
contracts." The one-language decision took thirty-seven paper applications,
a three-family panel, a spelling spike and an author ruling (LLP
0329.014.004 §9, LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b) to close, and the shipped result is
the most readable artifact in Snapback 2: the chat schema is 129 lines and a
query is three to six. Reopening the language will consume the first month.
What *should* be reopened is the bound model under it, which §6 phrases as a
comparison to "concentrate early investigation on." Half of the Quarry
refusals a builder meets are the certifier's byte and step prices wearing
Quarry syntax; the `fn`/`proc` split and the page-to-list gap (LLP 1018.008
F4) are bounded-composition restrictions the pricing model forced. Decide
the bound model (§6), keep the language, and re-derive its restrictions.

### 4.2 The first slice is at the scale where Snapback 2 looked elegant

§10 proposes "a private conversation with paginated messages and an unread
count," "two principals and two clients." Snapback 2 passed that slice in
its first week. It broke at the hub: 1,000 memberships refused the digest
fold, made global search cost a thousand requests, and refused the
envelopes builders first wrote (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). Every one of those
was discovered by a cold builder on a published package weeks after the
architecture was fixed. The first slice must include the hub principal from
day one (§11).

### 4.3 It investigates the client data model; the field has answered

§6 says "test a canonical local store with deliberate derived indexes,
durable pending operations, and explicit coverage and authorization
knowledge." That sentence describes a replica; the field has shipped it
under several names (buckets, mutators and a change stream, per-device
queues), Snapback 2's own LLP 1012 §6 reaches it by the hard road ("the
coverage rule that makes everything on the phone exact" is a fully drained
partition), and LLP 1019's `deliver` already computes the audience the
engine must maintain. Decide it (§7) and spend the investigation budget on
the two things the field has *not* settled for this design: partition
inversion of Quarry rules, and horizons at hub scale.

### 4.4 "Builder, human or agent" is two audiences

§3's first row conflates them. Agents need structured refusals with a
  invalidated by a clamshell sleep on battery mid-run (23:10Z–01:57Z), which
  stretched `ordinary-search-oracle.test.mjs` and failed it. Run alone on AC with
  the lid open, that file passed 8/8.

The 0.4.33 Mac gate now watches AC power, the lid and sleep during the run, and
restarts an attempt it marks environment-invalid. Details:
[reports/20261017-publication-0.4.31/REPORT.md](reports/20261017-publication-0.4.31/REPORT.md).

Integration 18 upgrade correction: retained executable identity ignores the
compiler-generated device compatibility report, whose server version changes
on a CLI upgrade. The full control-request digest still covers that report;
activation recompiles/rechecks the unchanged retained source. An actual 0.4.33
store with declared privacy intent and rollback history exposed this mismatch.
The focused unit regression distinguishes identity from integrity and preserves
source/native/worker byte changes as new executable content. Proof hosts also
honor their explicit binary environment so qualification cannot accidentally
select a stale checkout release binary.

## 2026-10-19 — SEC4 independent intent and default verification

LLP 3000.036.004 §13/13.5 has Muse and fresh Codex READY design reviews.
SEC4 replaces taught rule copies with spec action/role intent, advises equivalent
invariants, synthesizes typed per-role initial fixtures, labels generated origins,
and adds update-only column intent. Check has a compact note block and bounded
concrete work; test/audit retain full campaigns. Source evidence is read-only;
per-lane measurements and qualification are in reports/20261019-sec4.
DX4 owns staging/upload/dev papercuts; QRY owns read return and hidden lookups.

## 2026-10-19 — SEC4 wave-19 correction

Merged origin/main before changes. Coordinator wave-19 addendum supersedes the
seed-only demotion: authored fixture read/integrity violations fail. Empty
fixtures generate; payload initialization executes authored creators, preserving
requires, principal-role checks and copied values. Exact-source/fixture/CLI
positive evidence carries test refutations into bounded check without cached
proof. Escalation notes bypass the summary cap, exposure counts are separate,
and rule-changing deployment prints a matching-campaign demand. Real role and
relationship transitions retain their committed history. LLP 3000.036.004 §13.7
and wave-19 evidence/report record reviews, qualification and remaining gaps.
Column read rules remain outside SEC4; release visibility belongs to its owner.

Wave-19 follow-up: distinct equal-length histories exposed both real linkUnit
and grantRole → read paths. Counterfactual column mutation must not break an
immutable creator copy; actual typed payloads remain observable. Independent
review reproduced source-change-during-test and followed-symlink fingerprint
bugs. Tests now consume captured source strings and private fixture snapshots,
including symlink target bytes; changed originals prevent persistence. Those
exact regressions pass. Optional role columns retain two active actors per role.

Wave19 independent implementation review also reproduced a post-insert require
through the returned row alias. Paired column independence now follows alias
dependencies into require/check/if guards and declines unresolved coupling;
the exact safe transaction has a regression. Saved refutation records deduplicate
initial observations while retaining complete supporting rows, including the
600-row regression. Packed policy qualification counts actual native or HTTP
challenges separately and retains the existing ephemeral-fork coverage gap.

Packed social follow-up supersedes the earlier ephemeral-fork limitation:
fixture copies now retain independent hub rows/indexes, deadlines, quota
accounting/limits and epoch/ring state under the hub lock, without a publisher.
A mismatched store generation refuses. Durable campaigns run in mixed schemas;
ephemeral mutation histories and external presence are still explicit gaps.
The core regression verifies copied rows, deadlines, global capacity, independent
delete/write/sweep, unchanged live publisher and generation mismatch refusal.
The exact social starter also has a native campaign regression.
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

exec
/bin/bash -lc "timeout 25s bash -c 'git show HEAD:llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md | sed -n \"/"'^## 13/,$ p"'"'" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4

## 13. SEC4: independent intent and usable default campaigns (2026-10-19)

Implementer: Codex, SEC4, 2026-10-19. Wave 18 found 192/231 row
invariants copied enforcement, no audit use, and one campaign run. DX4 owns
staging discovery/provisioning and QRY owns query return/hidden lookup semantics.

### 13.1 Intent and diagnostics

An advisory N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE identifies table/column effect intent
whose bound predicate and effective gate imply each other in the existing
three-valued kernel. Equality is advice only: neither the note nor a generated
candidate establishes independent specification intent. Keep existing valid
assertions and proof statuses; do not reject a legitimate simple owner policy.
Suggest reviewing the intended role, resource relationship, sensitive column
and lifecycle condition. Draft candidates are explicitly unconfirmed questions
with per-role/per-column policy alternatives, never copies of the effective
gate. Where no independent candidate is available, emit a question rather than
inventing intent. Starters explain the product requirement and show a distinct
policy decomposition (for example member/admin alternatives).

### 13.2 Generated initial worlds and password-only test

Without explicit fixture files, generate isolated deterministic typed fixtures:
two distinct principals per discovered role and service seat, related resource,
membership and ownership rows, independent principal fields, enum alternatives,
and verified image/file uploads. Ordinary seed validation, constraints, defaults,
maintenance and reference ordering remain mandatory. Declined populations name
their coverage gap; continue with the validated empty world if no population
can be admitted. Existing explicit fixtures retain their meaning.

GeneratedFixtureTrace is distinct from AuthoredFixtureTrace: it establishes an
actual authored operation/read from a validated generated initial fixture,
**not** an application operation sequence that creates that initial fixture.
A replayed policy violation from either fixture origin fails acceptance. Seed-
only and synthetic table probes remain supplemental/non-failing. Every trace
retains the full initial rows and actual arguments; reports label this origin.
The same test-only in-memory engine path works with password-only schemas;
never open development doors or change deployed identity configuration. HTTP
authentication stays separately exercised through the existing isolated serve.
A fixture-free default test may pass its generated campaigns without pretending
that application business cases ran. Saved-source and explicit business-case
requirements retain their existing contract.

### 13.3 Coverage and bounded checking

Column update intent is spelled `invariant table.column update only P`; existing
`write` still includes insert and update. Multi-write observations may judge a
unique explicit target write when other stable writes touch different tables;
ambiguous repeated target writes and unstable policy dependencies stay unproved.
Histories interleave actors, argument alternatives, relationship changes and
successor operations. Media disclosure uses compiler-typed physical payload
witnesses; alternate-world media comparisons need a coupled verified ledger.
Role-wide branches gated by row values receive advisory N_ROLE_WIDE_READ when
the table carries private intent or media; suggest a projection/board table.
Escalation advice must constrain the assigned role by dominating argument guards
before naming it; an excluded target is not a witness.

`check` performs the sound static analysis and a bounded concrete campaign;
work exhaustion is explicit unproved coverage and points to `test`. A shared
short campaign deadline bounds exploration, including observer/world loops.
Already replayed violations remain failures; timeout never awards proof.
Memoizing exact implication input pairs is allowed; the cache is bounded,
process-local, and stores only the existing kernel's result. No assumptions
about fixture rows enter static proofs. `test` and `audit` retain the full
campaign bounds. Target cold/warm median check latency is at most five seconds
on the fifty wave-18 schemas.

Default check output is one short summary block with invariant counts and at
most five ranked actionable notes with source lines and concrete next steps.
Full reasons, individual unproved paths and complete note lists require
`--verbose` (JSON retains complete structured evidence). Refuted reachable
witnesses remain visible in compact form. Recommend `snapback4 test` when
exploration remains and `snapback4 audit` when exposures need review. The
start packet/cheatsheet teach check → test → audit as the ordinary loop.

### 13.4 Qualification

Pin before/after measurements, all fifty fixture-free campaign outcomes, the
eleven high-lane spec-intent outcomes, and the 200-schema no-false-refusal
survey. Preserve frozen lane sources. Run changed snippets/starters through
packed CLI qualification, the full requested crate/client/differential suites,
Linux qualification, affected Mac tests, wasm/ref/docs/root checks. Reports
distinguish actual caught, advisory/partial, and uncovered findings. No release
version bump, push or publication belongs to SEC4.

### 13.5 Review clarifications (revision 2)

This amendment supersedes §12.2's authored-fixture-only failure rule: both
fixture origins fail on replayed authored observations, with explicit origin in
every compact failure line, JSON, counterexample selection and count. Generated
initialization remains a trusted test assumption, not proof of empty-state
reachability. Synthetic table operations and trusted-seed-only assertions still
do not fail. `apply` prefers an authored fixture trace, then a generated fixture
trace, then supplemental witnesses; retain the shortest trace within each origin.
Qualification must analyze each new generated-origin refusal, including schemas
with no authored invariants. No generated coverage yields Proved or a zero-hole
claim. The 200-schema survey defines a false refusal as a new refusal without a
replayed violation of independently confirmed intent (invariant-free schemas
must have zero new refusals). Pin each high lane's source hash and caught/partial/
not-caught finding table, and each fixture-free campaign's result and origin.

A default fixture-free test with no saved-source selection clears `no_cases`
only after its generated campaigns execute; it always prints `not covered: no
authored business cases; generated-fixture only`. Explicit fixtures and saved
sources retain the business-case rule; `--fixture-only` keeps its meaning.

Check has a shared deterministic 512 call/world reservation budget, in addition
to a 1500 ms safety deadline. Each generated world, history replay step and
observer call reserves a unit (paired observers reserve their four calls).
Existing full-campaign caps remain upper bounds. Ordering is deterministic,
with initial authored typed read observers and column pairs before history
prefixes, then remaining synthetic worlds. The test-only operation matrix remains excluded
from check; `test` and `audit` use full campaign bounds. Exhaustion names `check
concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run
snapback4 test for the full campaign` (or `time budget exhausted`). Counts and
complete uncovered details remain in JSON/verbose. No operation result or policy
judgment cache is added: only exact full RuleExpr implication input pairs are
memoized process-locally, bounded to 4096 entries.

The compact block always counts proved/refuted/unproved, labels supplemental
seed/synthetic evidence non-failing, and lists **all** failing invariant sites
with fixture origin and one operation. Notes sort by existing note_rank tier, then security consequence (escalation,
role-wide disclosure, other correctness, mirror, broad-read, missing intent),
then site/code/message, show at most five, and print an explicit remainder count
with `--verbose`/JSON. It must never say pass/ok beside a failing exit.

The mirror comparison uses the row read AND applicable private-owner gate for
column reads, the insert/delete rule for those effects and the judged update
gate for updates. Both directions require proved implication on Readable;
unknown/cap exhaustion emits no equivalence note. This replaces §10.3's
rule-copy draft guidance. Current enforcement remains available in audit; the
draft contains only independent unconfirmed alternatives and spec questions.
Column update grammar is `invariant table.column update only P`: update arm
only, both old and next images, with no whole-table syntactic coverage. Column
write still covers insert plus update. Multi-write reductions retain the full
§12.7/12.8 transitive implicit-target and policy stability closure.

### 13.6 Implementation qualification clarifications

Fixture-free execution retains both empty and generated initial states. The
empty state gets 64 single/pair history slots and the populated state gets 448,
sharing the existing total 512 singles/512 pairs plus observer reservations.
Empty-start traces retain AuthoredFixtureTrace because no synthetic initial
rows are assumed. Related grant/membership/ownership tables include every
sampled actor; payload tables need independent owner/resource rows and enum/
optional alternatives. Optional time/relationship values include null. Text
arguments include typed authored comparison literals; integer arguments include
bounds/changed values. Candidate ordering interleaves domains before truncation.
No new runtime allowance, query result cache or policy judgment cache is added.

Column paired observers prune only programs outside the transitive maintained,
through-parent and raw-rule observer closure; every retained experiment still
validates both worlds, fixed arguments, policy falsehood and repeatability.
Image/file alternatives upload into one fixture ledger fork shared by both
worlds. Once/immutable media uses a fresh validated store with that ledger.
Ambiguous/unsupported paths remain named, never proved. Initial disclosure
witnesses cannot be replaced by unrelated later prefixes.

The corpus survey reports original source exits separately from intent-quality
corrections on disposable copies: an omitted authorized service seat and a
private whole-row declaration over intentionally public occupancy totals are
declared-policy contradictions, not reasons to silently exempt seats or ignore
aggregate observations. The latter requires column privacy intent; the former
requires the intended seat explicitly. Preserve original hashes, exact witnesses,
corrections and both outcomes. Do not describe original-source refusals as all
green or infer an application vulnerability solely from these generated states.

The isolated production HTTP preparation also covers schemas with live
read-through. Preview population captures live in the disposable snapshot's
main database: activation may switch SQLite's temp store, which drops TEMP
tables. This preserves the existing before/after comparison; no live release
state or activation semantics change.

Typed payload recognition includes concrete rows already returned by an
incomplete page: incompleteness cannot undo a disclosure. Repeatability and
actual compiler-typed field identity are still required. Paired absence/boolean
comparisons retain their completeness restrictions. Every history absence world,
including a ledger reconstruction, revalidates cross-table final-exists before
comparison. Compact advisory headers make no claim that the whole check passed.
QRY owns changes to partial-return execution and hidden lookup semantics.

Implicit-write stability distinguishes affected tables from possibly deleted
tables. Plain updates do not fire on-delete cascades. Explicit deletes and
expiring written tables may delete; maintained targets and live read-through
retirement conservatively propagate possible deletion. Cascade actions continue
the deletion closure, while set-null propagates writes (and expiry if present).
This permits the upheld-appeal update intent of wave-18 033 without discarding
its appeal predicate merely because bouts declare an unrelated delete cascade.
No runtime deletion or cascade semantics change.

Expiring writes use the runtime's `is_expiring()` classification, including
inherited read-through deadlines and ephemeral lifetimes. Replacing an expired
identity may retire its predecessor and cascade approvals before later guards;
that dependency is never treated as stable just because authored `expire` is absent.


### 13.7 Wave-19 correction: admissible starts and persistent refutations

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-19. This section supersedes conflicting seed-only,
fixture-selection and bounded-check wording above. The coordinator explicitly
commissions this semantic correction after wave-19 day-two evidence.

An absent or row-empty fixture selection uses generated security populations;
an empty directory is not an authored population. Invalid/nonempty explicit
fixtures still refuse. Business-case and saved-source selection retain their
separate meanings. Authored fixture assertions and admitted row-read witnesses
fail even without an authored initialization trace. They are trusted starting
assumptions, not synthetic writes. Arbitrarily invented schema snapshots and
unreachable synthetic writes remain supplemental. Column disclosure still needs
an actual authored observer; raw whole-row admission cannot refute a projected
private field.

Generated candidates are proposals, not trusted fixtures. Bootstrap roster rows
and tables without an authored creator must satisfy an ordinary insert gate for
an identified actor, defaults and all constraints. Where authored creators
exist, generate initial payloads by executing those creators with real typed
arguments and uploaded media, preserving their requires, copied relationships,
constant/default fields and role restrictions. Never independently randomize
values a creator couples. Unsupported creators/populations name their gap;
missing coverage is not proof. Actual later role changes and relationship
transfers retain their committed steps and generated or authored fixture origin.
Historical grants are not silently repaired to the current role/relationship.

The bounded check cannot promise to rediscover every full campaign witness.
`test` records only its concrete refutations and completion metadata, keyed to
all selected source bytes, fixture input bytes and the exact CLI executable.
`check` incorporates matching refutations into its ordinary failing summary;
changed source, fixtures or binary invalidate the record. No cached passing
verdict establishes proof. Persistence failure is explicit. The record lives
with generated project artifacts, not a deployed store or authentication state.

Every escalation note remains visible in short output even beyond the ordinary
five-note cap. Risk names prefer the role conferring the largest established
surface, without relying on names such as admin. Check prints a short exposure
line independent of N_ note rendering. A rule-changing deploy --check explicitly
demands `snapback4 test` when no matching completed campaign exists, including
when no independent invariants are declared. It prints the short exposure
summary; it must not silently imply that changed rules were security-tested.
The demand is actionable preflight output, not a new prohibition on activation.

Negative-existence column policies use actual current snapshot truth, never
negate an unavailable/unknown probe into a refutation. Column update intent
observes only actual changes to that column; changing a sibling is not evidence.
Finite campaign caps and unknown stability boundaries remain explicit. Tests
must cover the wave-19 examples (041 mismatched grant role, 044 assignee,
034 judge/coach overlap, 047 cold-approval state, 019 copied immutable owner),
not suppress findings by weakening independent intent.


Wave-19 review clarifications (controlling): the coordinator explicitly requires
catches from the app's own seed/fixtures to fail again. An authored stale seed
therefore fails under its trusted starting-state assumption; it is never
credited as an observed role-change or transfer. Credit for 019's transition
coverage requires the committed role/relationship mutation in the same trace,
followed by the actual forbidden observer. Generated initial states never gain
that credit merely by arranging stale fields. Partial nonempty authored fixtures
are retained as authored input without silently supplementing invented rows;
row-empty inputs use generation, and missing populations remain named coverage.

Creator discovery uses the bound mutation IR's insert/upsert write census. Its
actual interpreter executes require predicates and copied fields; no symbolic
coupling guess admits a generated payload. Global or explicitly role-bearing
roster proposals and tables without an authored creator require a specific
insert-admitted actor in the pre-insert world; a candidate cannot authorize its
own bootstrap. Text arguments include literals compared in bound binary
expressions (including require role='operator'). Generated starting states are
still explicitly labeled; initialization is never silently called empty-start.

The record covers the fixed full-default security campaign (512 single/pair
history envelope, existing full observer/matrix caps and clock NOW), not a
caller-selected bounded campaign. Exact executable identity binds these
constants and their algorithms; source and sorted fixture names/contents bind
inputs. JSON, verbosity, business-case flags and sweep --as do not change this
security campaign; residency selections do not write records; saved-source tests bind selected
source and contemporary fixture bytes separately from the working source.
Records include attempted/sequence counts and coverage-gap counts. Completion
means all finite security phases returned, not exhaustive coverage or proof;
authentication-boundary failure cannot produce completed evidence. No passing
status is loaded into static proof. Write failure is explicit and leaves the
preview demanding a campaign. Records are generated/security-test.json under
the app's snapback directory; temp-file rename avoids partial reads.

Changed/new/deleted table rules count as rule changes, comparing all four bound
gates against the currently selected release. Preview prints the demand even
with zero invariants; it does not veto actual activation. SEC4 also surfaces
this reminder on ordinary deploy. The short exposure line states table,
conditional-audience and broad-read counts independently of N_ note rendering;
audit retains exact per-role and per-column predicates. Most-privileged naming
sorts candidate roles by the number of table/effect gates proved universally
admitted after substituting that role, descending, then deterministic role
order; the source location, effective gate and recipe remain in the note.
The existing 200-schema false-refusal bar and explicit origin labels remain.

Only failure-eligible origins are persisted or reloaded, with their exact origin
and witness. Supplemental synthetic observations are never promoted by caching.
A later witness-free or interrupted run on identical inputs does not erase
established failures: records merge prior eligible witnesses, deduplicating by
site/observer/viewer/effect/origin; a source, fixture or executable change is
the invalidation boundary. Full phase completion and known violations are
separate metadata. Saved-source deploys cannot borrow the working-source record;
the demand must name the corresponding `test --source <candidate> --fixture ...`
selection, and use matching selected-source evidence, never the working-source record.

Distinct committed operation/viewer paths are not collapsed merely because
their lengths match. A one-step foundUnit witness cannot hide registerStaff,
and a one-step provision cannot hide linkUnit. Deduplication preserves each
operation/viewer path; repeated values on that path remain finite sampling.
A declined duplicate never mutates a previous witness's operation or arguments.
Relationship candidates prioritize actual admitted principal transfers from the
initial world, with explicit bounded probing, followed by ordinary replay and
observation. No preflight-only candidate is itself a refutation.

Retained-grant lifetime advice no longer requires declared role intent when a
grant writer depends on a mutable role roster but the read branch does not
consult that roster. Its wording is conditional: grants can survive role
changes; confirm whether current-role intent is required. This does not infer
a privacy specification or refute an invariant. It restores 019's pointer while
keeping lifetime advice out of the escalation/failing-witness tier.

A bounded check with matching known failures may skip additional concrete
resampling. It still runs static analysis and advice, incorporates the eligible
witnesses, names the skipped coverage and fails. Other obligations stay unproved;
full audit/test retain their campaigns. This avoids paying both executable
fingerprinting and exploratory work merely to rediscover a known failure.

Counterfactual column worlds must also respect authored coupling. A field
produced only by copied/computed/constant writer expressions, or by an argument
that is additionally used in a guard/another expression, is not independently
perturbed. The paired reduction stays uncovered unless a bound writer supplies
it from an independently used typed argument. Actual compiler-typed payloads
and committed transition observations still run and can fail. In particular,
changing immutable reports.homeowner while leaving its source chimney.owner
fixed cannot make a safe self-filtered query appear to leak a private field.

Records bind the bytes actually consumed, not a fresh read after testing. Source
strings are captured before compilation. Selected fixture inputs, including
symlink targets followed by the loader, are copied once into private disposable
scratch and both business/security phases consume that copy. The key is derived
from those captured source/fixture bytes. Before publishing the record, compare
the current original selection to that key; a change is explicit stale-input
evidence and does not persist. Later fixture symlink-target changes invalidate
records exactly like ordinary file edits. Scratch is removed on every return.

Writer-object coupling follows compiler Const/Let aliases (ordinary inserts
write a temporary, not an inline object). Unknown/reassigned object aliases are
not independent column worlds. Raw trusted row admission needs one witness per
declaration, not a full cloned initial snapshot for every row. Persisted evidence
deduplicates on the first write as well as later merges, keeping the complete
chosen initial world. A successfully stored matching refutation is never silently
ignored because its JSON exceeds a size threshold. The 600-row regression retains
all initial rows in a sub-megabyte record and check still fails on that evidence.

Guard dependence includes aliases of inserted rows and subsequent reads of the
written table, including post-insert require/check rollback. An argument's
single textual occurrence does not establish independence when a guard observes
its stored value through such an alias; unresolved guard aliases stay uncovered.
Packed qualification requires an actual policy or isolated HTTP challenge,
records the two counts separately, and retains explicit native campaign gaps
(e.g. the existing unsupported ephemeral-hub fork) rather than claiming native
mutation coverage from HTTP probes.

Packed social qualification exposed a complete native campaign skip when an
otherwise durable app merely declared an ephemeral table. Fixture-only forks
now copy the hub under its lock into independent memory, preserving row/index
state, deadlines, per-principal/global quota accounting and limits, epoch, ring
and generation binding. The copy never retains the live publisher or external
presence. A mismatched hub/store generation refuses. These are quiescent test
fixture copies, not live adoption or wire changes (LLP3000.017 §§3–4).
Durable generated initialization and histories then run normally in mixed apps.
Generated ephemeral populations and ephemeral mutation histories remain explicit
coverage gaps; their mutation programs are skipped rather than dispatched
through the durable mutation door. External presence still refuses a fixture
fork. Packed social must exercise actual native challenges, not a zero-work pass.

The unsupported ephemeral-program boundary applies in every dispatch phase,
including column/row paired observers and foreign-asset challenges, with a
central observer refusal as a backstop. No phase credits a skipped ephemeral
mutation as a reachable witness. Durable query controls still execute.
Column independence follows aliases into other written fields/tables as well
as guards. A creator copying an inserted private value into a related marker
constrains the alternate world; changing only the source value cannot turn
an always-public id list into a column disclosure. Alias-dependent operators
and opaque effects likewise stay conservatively uncovered.

Alias dependence is field-sensitive across compiler-owned object projections:
a copied row id does not couple an independently supplied private payload.
Unknown, defaulted or implicit upsert field sources remain uncovered. An
immutable-column alternate that must rebuild the SQL fixture declines when
the original hub has committed ephemeral state; it must not reset that state
and attribute the resulting public-presence difference to a protected column.
Actual typed disclosures and state-preserving forks remain available.

exec
